--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed such that the two black nodes (H and O) are completely disconnected, we should first identify and enumerate all paths between these nodes via their neighboring nodes and edges.

**Identification of Neighbors and Paths:**

1. **Starting from node H:**
   - Direct neighbors: G, E, B

2. **Starting from node O:**
   - Direct neighbors: P, C, Y

To find all paths, we need to track back from each of the direct neighbors of H and O to see their paths converge:

- **From H to E:**
  - Path: H -> E -> M -> L
  - Path: H -> E -> B -> C -> N -> Y (Does not lead to O)

- **From H to B:**
  - Path: H -> B -> E -> L
  - Path: H -> B -> C -> N -> Y
  - Path: H -> B -> C -> O (Direct path)

- **From H to G:**
  - Path: H -> G -> X -> F -> E -> M -> L
  - Path: H -> G -> X -> F -> E -> B -> C -> N -> Y
  - Path: H -> G -> X -> F -> E -> B -> C -> O 

- **From O to C:**
  - Path: O -> C -> B -> E -> M -> L
  - Path: O -> C -> B -> E -> H
  - Path: O -> C -> B -> H
  - Path: O -> C -> N -> Y

- **From O to P:**
  - Path: O -> P -> I -> B -> E -> M -> L
  - Path: O -> P -> I -> B -> E -> H
  - Path: O -> P -> I -> H
  - Path: O -> P -> D (Does not lead to H)

- **From O to Y:**
  - Path: O -> Y -> N -> C -> B -> E -> M -> L
  - Path: O -> Y -> N -> C -> B -> E -> H
  - Path: O -> Y -> N -> C -> B -> H
  - Path: O -> Y -> N -> C -> O (No new paths as cyclic)

From the above analysis, the convergence points are at nodes E, B, C. Thus, removing any one of these won’t be sufficient, as there are alternate paths through the others. Therefore, the minimum set is the nodes where most paths converge:

**Final Cut Set:**

Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [B, C]

By removing nodes B and C, the direct paths linking H and O through various combinations are all severed, ensuring a complete disconnection between the black nodes H and O.
--- Ground Truth ---
{'length': 3, 'cut_set': ['F', 'B', 'I']}
